- Pass: remote ran suite, passed\n22\t/// - TestFailure: remote ran suite, failed (tests, compilation, etc.)\n23\t/// - GateFailure: an enabled quality gate failed while tests passed\n24\t/// - InfraFailure: no verdict produced (push/submit/schedule/stream/deadline issues)\n25\t/// - Cancelled: user cancelled the run (Ctrl-C)\n26\t/// - Superseded: a newer sha for the same (repo, args) cancelled this run (v1.x)\n27\t///\n28\t/// The key invariant (INV-7): TestFailure and GateFailure NEVER trigger local retry.\n29\t/// Only InfraFailure triggers capped local fallback.\n30\t#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\n31\tpub enum Verdict {\n32\t    /// The remote ran the suite and it passed.\n33\t    Pass,\n34\t    /// The remote ran the suite and it failed (tests, compilation, etc.).\n35\t    TestFailure {\n36\t        /// The actual exit code from the test run (preserved for faithful exit code).\n37\t        exit_code: i32,\n38\t    },\n39\t    /// An (INV-7).\n43\t    GateFailure {\n44\t        /// Exit code from the gate check.\n45\t        exit_code: i32,\n46\t        /// Which gate failed (e.g., \"clippy\", \"fmt\").\n47\t        gate_name: client deadline exceeded, pod OOMKilled, workflow deadline exceeded, etc.\n53\t    /// Triggers capped local fallback.\n54\t    InfraFailure {\n55\t        /// Human-readable reason for the >= 2 -> InfraFailure (submit/schedule/infra issues)\n74\t    ///\n75\t    /// This matches the documented command backend contract where the remote\n76\t    /// executor classifies failures via exit code.\n77\t    ///\n78\t    /// For Argo, the verdict comes from status.phase and verdict.json, not from\n79\t    /// kubectl's exit code, so this is used primarily for the command backend.\n80\t    pub fn from_exit_code(code: i32) -> Self {\n81\t        match code {\n82\t            0 => Verdict::Pass,\n83\t            1 => Verdict::TestFailure { exit_code: 1 },\n84\t            _ => Verdict::InfraFailure {\n85\t                reason: format!(\"backend exit code {}\", code),\n86\t            },\n87\t        }\n88\t    }\n89\t\n90\t    /// Convert the verdict to an exit code for the caller.\n91\t    ///\n92\t    /// Phase 1a:\n93\t    /// - Pass -> 0\n94\t    /// - TestFailure -> preserved exit code\n95\t    /// - GateFailure -> preserved exit code (non-zero)\n96\t    /// - InfraFailure -> 1 (will trigger local fallback at caller)\n97\t    /// - Cancelled -> 130 (standard SIGINT exit code)\n98\t    /// - Superseded -> 0 (run didn't produce a verdict, not a failure)\n99\t    pub fn to_exit_code(self) -> i32 {\n100\t        match self {\n101\t            Verdict::Pass => 0,\n102\t            Verdict::TestFailure { exit_code } => exit_code.max(1), // Ensure at least 1\n103\t            Verdict::GateFailure { exit_code, .. } => exit_code.max(1),\n104\t            Verdict::InfraFailure { .. } => 1, // Will trigger local fallback\n105\t            Verdict::Cancelled => 130,         // Standard SIGINT exit code\n106\t            Verdict::Superseded { .. } => 0,   // Not a failure, just superseded\n107\t        }\n108\t    }\n109\t\n110\t    /// Check if this verdict should trigger a local fallback run.\n111\t    ///\n112\t    /// Only InfraFailure triggers local fallback per INV-7.\n113\t    /// TestFailure and GateFailure NEVER retry locally.\n114\t    pub fn should_fallback(&self) -> bool {\n115\t        matches!(self, Verdict::InfraFailure { .. })\n116\t    }\n117\t\n118\t    /// Create a test failure verdict from an exit code.\n119\t    pub fn test_failure(exit_code: i32) -> Self {\n120\t        Verdict::TestFailure { exit_code: exit_code.max(1) }\n121\t    }\n122\t\n123\t    /// Create a gate failure verdict.\n124\t    pub fn gate_failure(gate_name: String, exit_code: i32) -> Self {\n125\t        Verdict::GateFailure {\n126\t            gate_name,\n127\t            exit_code: exit_code.max(1),\n128\t        }\n129\t    }\n130\t\n131\t    /// Create an infra failure verdict with a reason.\n132\t    pub fn infra_failure(reason: String) -> Self {\n133\t        Verdict::InfraFailure { reason }\n134\t    }\n135\t}\n136\t\n137\timpl fmt::Display for Verdict {\n138\t    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n139\t        match self {\n140\t            Verdict::Pass => write!(f,         Verdict::InfraFailure { reason } => write!(f, \"InfraFailure({})\", reason),\n144\t            Verdict::Cancelled => write!(f, \"Cancelled\"),\n145\t            Verdict::Superseded { .. } => write!(f, \"Superseded\"),\n146\t        }\n147\t    }\n148\t}\n149\t\n150\t/// Error type for backend operations.\n151\t///\n152\t/// Phase 0.5: minimal string-based error type.\n153\t/// Phase 1a stream_logs(&self, h: &RunHandle, out: &mut dyn std::io::Write) -> Result<(), BackendError> {\n252\t        let _ = (h, out);\n253\t        panic!(\"stream_logs is not implemented in Phase 0.5\");\n254\t    }\n255\t\n256\t    /// Wait for the remote run to complete and return its verdict.\n257\t    ///\n258\t    /// This is the authoritative source for the run's outcome — log URLs or similar for Argo; command backends\n269\t    /// may return the handle or a configured URL template.\n270\t    fn describe(&self, h: &RunHandle) -> String {\n271\t        let _ = h;\n272\t        panic!(\"describe is not implemented in Phase 0.5\");\n273\t    }\n274\t\n275\t    /// Cancel a running run (Ctrl-C propagation).\n276\t    ///\n277\t    /// Phase 0.5: may panic — this is not out the SHA, and runs the\n290\t/// command in the specified directory (relative to repo root).\n291\t#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]\n292\tpub struct RunSpec {\n293\t    /// Tool name (e.g., It is red and attributed, and — like TestFailure — never retries\n475\t        // locally (plan §\"Verdict semantics\", INV-7).\n476\t        if let Some(gate_name) = self.gate_failed {\n477\t            return Verdict::gate_failure(gate_name, self.exit_code.max(1));\n478\t        }\n479\t\n480\t        // No classification at all: fall back to phase and exit code\n481\t        match self.phase.as_str() {\n482\t            \"Succeeded\" => Verdict::Pass,\n483\t            \"Failed\" | \"Error\" => Verdict::test_failure(self.exit_code.max(1)),\n484\t            _ => Verdict::infra_failure(format!(\"unknown phase: {}\", self.phase)),\n485\t        }\n486\t    }\n487\t}\n488\t\n489\t#[cfg(test)]\n490\tmod tests {\n491\t    use super::*;\n492\t\n493\t gantry — RemoteBackend trait and Verdict enum (plan §\"Phase 0.5\", Components §5).\n//\n// Phase 0.5 walking skeleton, stage 4 of 5 (bf-2vr): builds on stages 1-3 (shim/config\n// + GitGate + RefPusher) and implements the minimal RemoteBackend trait with a\n// command-template backend that runs a bash executor on the same box.\n//\n// This module defines:\n// - Verdict: the exit-code ladder for this phase (0 = Pass, nonzero = TestFailure)\n// - BackendError: minimal error type for backend operations\n// - RunHandle: opaque handle returned by submit() and consumed by wait()\n// - RemoteBackend trait: submit / stream_logs / wait / describe / cancel\n\nuse std::fmt;\nuse std::path::PathBuf;\nuse serde::Deserialize;\nuse serde_json::Value;\n\n/// Verdict: the terminal classification of a run (plan §\"Data models\", \"Verdict semantics\").\n///\n/// Phase 1a implements the full verdict ladder:\n/// - Pass: remote ran suite, passed\n/// - TestFailure: remote ran suite, failed (tests, compilation, etc.)\n/// - GateFailure: an enabled quality gate failed while tests passed\n/// - InfraFailure: no verdict produced (push/submit/schedule/stream/deadline issues)\n/// - Cancelled: user cancelled the run (Ctrl-C)\n/// - Superseded: a newer sha for the same (repo, args) cancelled this run (v1.x)\n///\n/// The key invariant (INV-7): TestFailure and GateFailure NEVER trigger local retry.\n/// Only InfraFailure triggers capped local fallback.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum Verdict {\n    /// The remote ran the suite and it passed.\n    Pass,\n    /// The remote ran the suite and it failed (tests, compilation, etc.).\n    TestFailure {\n        /// The actual exit code from the test run (preserved for faithful exit code).\n        exit_code: i32,\n    },\n    /// An enabled quality gate failed while tests passed.\n    ///\n    /// This is distinguished from TestFailure so agents can fix lints rather than tests.\n    /// Never triggers local retry (INV-7).\n    GateFailure {\n        /// Exit code from the gate check.\n        exit_code: i32,\n        /// Which gate failed (e.g., \"clippy\", \"fmt\").\n        gate_name: String,\n    },\n    /// No verdict produced due to infrastructure issues.\n    ///\n    /// This covers: push/submit failures, scheduling issues, stream interruption,\n    /// client deadline exceeded, pod OOMKilled, workflow deadline exceeded, etc.\n    /// Triggers capped local fallback.\n    InfraFailure {\n        /// Human-readable reason for the infrastructure failure.\n        reason: String,\n    },\n    /// User cancelled the run (Ctrl-C).\n    Cancelled,\n    /// A newer sha for the same (repo, args) cancelled this run (v1.x feature).\n    Superseded {\n        /// The newer sha that superseded this run.\n        newer_sha: String,\n    },\n}\n\nimpl Verdict {\n    /// Convert an exit code to a Verdict using the full ladder.\n    ///\n    /// Phase 1a: command backend exit code semantics:\n    /// - 0 -> Pass\n    /// - 1 -> TestFailure (tests failed)\n    /// - >= 2 -> InfraFailure (submit/schedule/infra issues)\n    ///\n    /// This matches the documented command backend contract where the remote\n    /// executor classifies failures via exit code.\n    ///\n    /// For Argo, the verdict comes from status.phase and verdict.json, not from\n    /// kubectl's exit code, so this is used primarily for the command backend.\n    pub fn from_exit_code(code: i32) -> Self {\n        match code {\n            0 => Verdict::Pass,\n            1 => Verdict::TestFailure { exit_code: 1 },\n            _ => Verdict::InfraFailure {\n                reason: format!(\"backend exit code {}\", code),\n            },\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Convert the verdict to an exit code for the caller.\n    ///\n    /// Phase 1a:\n    /// - Pass -> 0\n    /// - TestFailure -> preserved exit code\n    /// - GateFailure -> preserved exit code (non-zero)\n    /// - InfraFailure -> 1 (will trigger local fallback at caller)\n    /// - Cancelled -> 130 (standard SIGINT exit code)\n    /// - Superseded -> 0 (run didn't produce a verdict, not a failure)\n    pub fn to_exit_code(self) -> i32 {\n        match self {\n            Verdict::Pass => 0,\n            Verdict::TestFailure { exit_code } => exit_code.max(1), // Ensure at least 1\n            Verdict::GateFailure { exit_code, .. } => exit_code.max(1),\n            Verdict::InfraFailure { .. } => 1, // Will trigger local fallback\n            Verdict::Cancelled => 130,         // Standard SIGINT exit code\n            Verdict::Superseded { .. } => 0,   // Not a failure, just superseded\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Check if this verdict should trigger a local fallback run.\n    ///\n    /// Only InfraFailure triggers local fallback per INV-7.\n    /// TestFailure and GateFailure NEVER retry locally.\n    pub fn should_fallback(&self) -> bool {\n        matches!(self, Verdict::InfraFailure { .. })\n    }\n\n    /// Create a test failure verdict from an exit code.\n    pub fn test_failure(exit_code: i32) -> Self {\n        Verdict::TestFailure { exit_code: exit_code.max(1) }\n    }\n\n    /// Create a gate failure verdict.\n    pub fn gate_failure(gate_name: String, exit_code: i32) -> Self {\n        Verdict::GateFailure {\n            gate_name,\n            exit_code: exit_code.max(1),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Create an infra failure verdict with a reason.\n    pub fn infra_failure(reason: String) -> Self {\n        Verdict::InfraFailure { reason }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for Verdict {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        match self {\n            Verdict::Pass => write!(f, \"Pass\"),\n            Verdict::TestFailure { .. } => write!(f, \"TestFailure\"),\n            Verdict::GateFailure { gate_name, .. } => write!(f, \"GateFailure({})\", gate_name),\n            Verdict::InfraFailure { reason } => write!(f, \"InfraFailure({})\", reason),\n            Verdict::Cancelled => write!(f, \"Cancelled\"),\n            Verdict::Superseded { .. } => write!(f, \"Superseded\"),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Error type for backend operations.\n///\n/// Phase 0.5: minimal string-based error type.\n/// Phase 1a will expand this to include InfraFailure classification.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]\npub struct BackendError {\n    /// Human-readable reason for the error.\n    pub reason: String,\n}\n\nimpl BackendError {\n    /// Create a new BackendError with a reason.\n    pub fn new(reason: &str) -> Self {\n        BackendError {\n            reason: reason.to_string(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for BackendError {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        write!(f, \"{}\", self.reason)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl std::error::Error for BackendError {}\n\n/// RunHandle: opaque handle returned by submit() and consumed by wait().\n///\n/// Phase 1a: structured handle with backend-specific state.\n/// - Argo: workflow name and pod name for streaming/polling\n/// - Command: opaque handle string (stdout of submit command)\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]\npub enum RunHandle {\n    /// Argo Workflow handle with workflow name and optional pod name.\n    Argo {\n        /// Workflow name (e.g., \"gantry-x7k2p\").\n        workflow_name: String,\n        /// Pod name for log streaming (None if not yet created).\n        pod_name: Option<String>,\n    },\n    /// Command backend handle with opaque string identifier.\n    Command {\n        /// Opaque handle string (output of submit command's stdout).\n        handle: String,\n    },\n}\n\nimpl RunHandle {\n    /// Create a new Command handle from a string.\n    pub fn command(handle: &str) -> Self {\n        RunHandle::Command {\n            handle: handle.to_string(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Create a new Argo handle with workflow name.\n    pub fn argo(workflow_name: &str) -> Self {\n        RunHandle::Argo {\n            workflow_name: workflow_name.to_string(),\n            pod_name: None,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Set the pod name for an Argo handle (used when pod is created).\n    pub fn with_pod_name(self, pod_name: String) -> Self {\n        match self {\n            RunHandle::Argo {\n                workflow_name,\n                pod_name: _,\n            } => RunHandle::Argo {\n                workflow_name,\n                pod_name: Some(pod_name),\n            },\n            _ => self, // Ignore pod name setting for non-Argo handles\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// RemoteBackend trait: the interface all remote executors must implement.\n///\n/// Phase 0.5: only submit() and wait() need real bodies; stream_logs, describe,\n/// and cancel may panic. The command backend implements this trait using hardcoded\n/// argv arrays that invoke a local bash executor.\n///\n/// Phase 1a will add streaming, cancellation, and describe support; the Argo\n/// backend will implement the full trait.\npub trait RemoteBackend {\n    /// Submit a run to the remote executor.\n    ///\n    /// Returns a RunHandle that wait() can use to poll the result. The handle is\n    /// opaque to the caller — only the backend implementation knows its structure.\n    ///\n    /// Phase 0.5: submit runs the configured submit argv and captures its stdout\n    /// as the handle. Failure (argv not found, non-zero exit) returns Err.\n    fn submit(&self, spec: &RunSpec) -> Result<RunHandle, BackendError>;\n\n    /// Stream logs from the remote run to a writer (best-effort).\n    ///\n    /// Phase 0.5: may panic — this is not implemented in the skeleton.\n    /// Phase 1a will implement this for both command and Argo backends.\n    fn stream_logs(&self, h: &RunHandle, out: &mut dyn std::io::Write) -> Result<(), BackendError> {\n        let _ = (h, out);\n        panic!(\"stream_logs is not implemented in Phase 0.5\");\n    }\n\n    /// Wait for the remote run to complete and return its verdict.\n    ///\n    /// This is the authoritative source for the run's outcome — log streaming\n    /// is best-effort, but wait() must always return a real verdict or an error.\n    ///\n    /// Phase 0.5: wait runs the configured wait argv with the handle and maps\n    /// the exit code to a Verdict using the minimal ladder.\n    fn wait(&self, h: &RunHandle, deadline: std::time::Instant) -> Result<Verdict, BackendError>;\n\n    /// Describe a run for human consumption (e.g., a URL to view logs).\n    ///\n    /// Phase 0.5: may panic — this is not implemented in the skeleton.\n    /// Phase 1a will return workflow URLs or similar for Argo; command backends\n    /// may return the handle or a configured URL template.\n    fn describe(&self, h: &RunHandle) -> String {\n        let _ = h;\n        panic!(\"describe is not implemented in Phase 0.5\");\n    }\n\n    /// Cancel a running run (Ctrl-C propagation).\n    ///\n    /// Phase 0.5: may panic — this is not implemented in the skeleton.\n    /// Phase 1a will implement cancellation for both backends (kubectl delete\n    /// for Argo, a cancel argv for command templates).\n    fn cancel(&self, h: &RunHandle) -> Result<(), BackendError> {\n        let _ = h;\n        panic!(\"cancel is not implemented in Phase 0.5\");\n    }\n}\n\n/// RunSpec: the specification of a run to submit to the remote backend.\n///\n/// Phase 1a: complete struct with all fields needed for remote execution.\n/// The remote executor clones the repo, checks out the SHA, and runs the\n/// command in the specified directory (relative to repo root).\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]\npub struct RunSpec {\n    /// Tool name (e.g., \"cargo\").\n    pub tool: String,\n    /// Subcommand (e.g., \"test\").\n    pub subcommand: String,\n    /// Arguments to pass to the command (e.g., [\"--\", \"--nocapture\"]).\n    pub args: Vec<String>,\n    /// Repository URL (file:// for local testing, git@ or https:// for production).\n    pub repo_url: String,\n    /// Commit SHA to run (the content the executor checks out).\n    pub sha: String,\n    /// Caller's directory relative to repo root (for workspace-member support).\n    pub cwd_rel: PathBuf,\n}\n\nimpl RunSpec {\n    /// Create a new RunSpec with all fields.\n    pub fn new(\n        tool: &str,\n        subcommand: &str,\n        args: Vec<String>,\n        repo_url: &str,\n        sha: &str,\n        cwd_rel: PathBuf,\n    ) -> Self {\n        RunSpec {\n            tool: tool.to_string(),\n            subcommand: subcommand.to_string(),\n            args,\n            repo_url: repo_url.to_string(),\n            sha: sha.to_string(),\n            cwd_rel,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Create a placeholder RunSpec for argv substitution (used in logs/wait/cancel).\n    ///\n    /// This creates a minimal RunSpec with placeholder values for when we only need\n    /// to substitute {handle} and don't have the full RunSpec available.\n    pub fn placeholder() -> Self {\n        RunSpec {\n            tool: String::new(),\n            subcommand: String::new(),\n            args: Vec::new(),\n            repo_url: String::new(),\n            sha: String::new(),\n            cwd_rel: PathBuf::new(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Get the full command argv for the tool (tool, subcommand, and args).\n    ///\n    /// This is what gets sent to the remote executor as the argv to execute.\n    pub fn full_argv(&self) -> Vec<String> {\n        let mut argv = vec![self.tool.clone(), self.subcommand.clone()];\n        argv.extend(self.args.clone());\n        argv\n    }\n}\n\npub mod argo;\npub mod command;\n\n/// Versioned verdict.json output from remote executors.\n///\n/// This schema is emitted by remote executors (e.g., Argo Workflows) to provide\n/// structured verdict information beyond exit codes. Consumers ignore unknown fields\n/// (additive evolution). An absent verdict.json degrades gracefully to exit-code-only\n/// interpretation (command templates without verdict support).\n///\n/// Schema version 1 (Phase 1a):\n/// - schema_version: 1\n/// - phase: Succeeded/Failed/Error\n/// - exit_code: the test run's exit code\n/// - oom: true if pod was OOMKilled\n/// - deadline_exceeded: true if workflow exceeded its deadline\n/// - failure_class: optional classification (compile-error, test-failure, doctest, harness-panic)\n#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(rename_all = \"snake_case\")]\npub enum VerdictJsonFailureClass {\n    /// Compilation error (could not build).\n    CompileError,\n    /// Test failure (tests ran but failed).\n    TestFailure,\n    /// Doctest failure.\n    Doctest,\n    /// Test harness panic.\n    HarnessPanic,\n}\n\n/// Versioned verdict.json schema.\n#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]\npub struct VerdictJson {\n    /// Schema version (must be 1 for Phase 1a).\n    #[serde(rename = \"schema_version\")]\n    pub schema_version: u32,\n    /// Workflow phase (Succeeded/Failed/Error).\n    pub phase: String,\n    /// The test run's exit code.\n    #[serde(rename = \"exit_code\")]\n    pub exit_code: i32,\n    /// True if the pod was OOMKilled.\n    #[serde(default)]\n    pub oom: bool,\n    /// True if the workflow exceeded its deadline.\n    #[serde(rename = \"deadline_exceeded\", default)]\n    pub deadline_exceeded: bool,\n    /// Optional failure classification.\n    #[serde(rename = \"failure_class\", default)]\n    pub failure_class: Option<VerdictJsonFailureClass>,\n    /// Name of the enabled quality gate that failed, when one did.\n    ///\n    /// Present only when a gate (clippy, fmt, …) failed; the name is what the\n    /// verdict is attributed to, so an agent fixes the lint rather than the tests.\n    /// Absent for every executor that runs no gates — an omitted field is not a\n    /// gate pass, it is \"no gates ran\", and both degrade to the same exit-code\n    /// interpretation.\n    #[serde(rename = \"gate_failed\", default)]\n    pub gate_failed: Option<String>,\n    /// Additional fields (for future compatibility).\n    #[serde(flatten)]\n    pub extra: std::collections::HashMap<String, Value>,\n}\n\nimpl VerdictJson {\n    /// Parse verdict.json from a JSON string.\n    ///\n    /// Returns Err if the JSON is invalid or schema_version is unsupported.\n    /// Returns Ok(None) if the JSON is valid but represents a future schema version\n    /// (consumers ignore unknown fields, but schema_version must be recognized).\n    pub fn parse(json: &str) -> Result<Option<Self>, String> {\n        let parsed: serde_json::Result<Self> = serde_json::from_str(json);\n        match parsed {\n            Ok(verdict) => {\n                // Check schema version\n                match verdict.schema_version {\n                    1 => Ok(Some(verdict)),\n                    version => Err(format!(\"unsupported schema version: {}\", version)),\n                }\n            }\n            Err(e) => Err(format!(\"failed to parse verdict.json: {}\", e)),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Convert the verdict.json to a Verdict enum.\n    ///\n    /// This implements the full verdict ladder semantics, in precedence order:\n    /// - OOMKilled or deadline exceeded -> InfraFailure (an infrastructure cap\n    ///   firing says nothing about the code; reporting it as a test failure would\n    ///   fake a result)\n    /// - Any failure_class -> TestFailure (the suite itself is what failed)\n    /// - gate_failed with no failure_class -> GateFailure attributed to that gate\n    ///   (tests passed, an enabled gate did not)\n    /// - Succeeded -> Pass; Failed/Error with no classification -> TestFailure\n    /// - Any other phase -> InfraFailure (an unreadable status is not a verdict)\n    ///\n    /// Only the InfraFailure rungs ever reach a local run\n    /// ([`crate::decision::decide_retry`], INV-7).\n    pub fn to_verdict(self) -> Verdict {\n        // Check for infrastructure failures first (OOM or deadline)\n        if self.oom || self.deadline_exceeded {\n            return Verdict::infra_failure(if self.oom {\n                \"pod was OOMKilled\"\n            } else {\n                \"workflow deadline exceeded\"\n            }.to_string());\n        }\n\n        // Parse failure_class if present\n        if let Some(class) = self.failure_class {\n            match class {\n                // All failure classes are test failures\n                VerdictJsonFailureClass::CompileError |\n                VerdictJsonFailureClass::TestFailure |\n                VerdictJsonFailureClass::Doctest |\n                VerdictJsonFailureClass::HarnessPanic => {\n                    return Verdict::test_failure(self.exit_code.max(1));\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        // No failure_class: a named gate failure is the suite passing and a gate\n        // not. It is red and attributed, and — like TestFailure — never retries\n        // locally (plan §\"Verdict semantics\", INV-7).\n        if let Some(gate_name) = self.gate_failed {\n            return Verdict::gate_failure(gate_name, self.exit_code.max(1));\n        }\n\n        // No classification at all: fall back to phase and exit code\n        match self.phase.as_str() {\n            \"Succeeded\" => Verdict::Pass,\n            \"Failed\" | \"Error\" => Verdict::test_failure(self.exit_code.max(1)),\n            _ => Verdict::infra_failure(format!(\"unknown phase: {}\", self.phase)),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_verdict_json_parse_succeeded() {\n        let json = r#\"{\n            \"schema_version\": 1,\n            \"phase\": \"Succeeded\",\n            \"exit_code\": 0\n        }\"#;\n        let verdict = VerdictJson::parse(json).unwrap().unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(verdict.schema_version, 1);\n        assert_eq!(verdict.phase, \"Succeeded\");\n        assert_eq!(verdict.exit_code, 0);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_verdict_json_parse_failed_with_class() {\n        let json = r#\"{\n            \"schema_version\": 1,\n            \"phase\": \"Failed\",\n            \"exit_code\": 1,\n            \"failure_class\": \"test_failure\"\n        }\"#;\n        let verdict = VerdictJson::parse(json).unwrap().unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(verdict.phase, \"Failed\");\n        assert!(matches!(verdict.failure_class, Some(VerdictJsonFailureClass::TestFailure)));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_verdict_json_parse_oom() {\n        let json = r#\"{\n            \"schema_version\": 1,\n            \"phase\": \"Failed\",\n            \"exit_code\": 137,\n            \"oom\": true\n        }\"#;\n        let verdict = VerdictJson::parse(json).unwrap().unwrap();\n        assert!(verdict.oom);\n        assert_eq!(verdict.to_verdict(), Verdict::infra_failure(\"pod was OOMKilled\".to_string()));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_verdict_json_parse_deadline_exceeded() {\n        let json = r#\"{\n            \"schema_version\": 1,\n            \"phase\": \"Failed\",\n            \"exit_code\": 1,\n            \"deadline_exceeded\": true\n        }\"#;\n        let verdict = VerdictJson::parse(json).unwrap().unwrap();\n        assert!(verdict.deadline_exceeded);\n        assert_eq!(verdict.to_verdict(), Verdict::infra_failure(\"workflow deadline exceeded\".to_string()));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_verdict_json_parse_unknown_field_ignored() {\n        let json = r#\"{\n            \"schema_version\": 1,\n            \"phase\": \"Succeeded\",\n            \"exit_code\": 0,\n            \"future_field\": \"some_value\"\n        }\"#;\n        let verdict = VerdictJson::parse(json).unwrap().unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(verdict.to_verdict(), Verdict::Pass);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_verdict_json_parse_unsupported_version() {\n        let json = The ladder is\n6\t// the load-bearing safety property of the whole tool:\n7\t//\n8\t//   Pass          → exit 0.\n9\t//   TestFailure   → exit non-zero, full stop. NEVER a local rerun (INV-7).\n10\t//   GateFailure   → exit non-zero, attributed to the named gate. NEVER a local\n11\t//                   rerun (INV-7) — an agent must fix the lint, not reroll dice.\n12\t//   InfraFailure  → no verdict was produced, so produce one locally: a capped\n13\t//                   local run whose exit code wins (DD-4).\n14\t//   Cancelled     → exit 130, no rerun (the user asked for it to stop).\n15\t//   Superseded    → a newer sha owns the answer; no rerun.\n16\t//\n17\t// \"Capped\" has two independent meanings in the plan and both are honored here as\n18\t// far as this phase reaches: the *attempt* cap lives in [`FallbackPolicy`] (a\n19\t// remote outage degrades to at most N local runs per invocation, default 1 — an\n20\t// infra failure can never become a retry loop), and the *resource* cap (cgroup\n21\t// slice, DD-9) plus the fallback admission semaphore land in their own Phase 1b\n22\t// beads (bf-xj0, bf-299) and hook into [`run_local`] when they do.\n23\t//\n24\t// Every rung logs why it was taken: \"silently skipped a test run\" must be an\n25\t// unrepresentable state (DD-4).\n26\t\n27\tuse crate::backend::command::CommandBackend;\n28\tuse crate::backend::{RemoteBackend, RunHandle, RunSpec, Verdict};\n29\tuse crate::config::Config;\n30\tuse crate::refs::RefPusher;\n31\tuse crate::shim;\n32\tuse std::path::PathBuf;\n33\tuse std::time::Instant;\n34\t\n35\t/// How many local runs an infra failure may degrade into, per invocation.\n36\t///\n37\t/// One: an infra failure buys exactly one honest local answer. Zero would make a\n38\t/// backend outage block builds; more than one would turn a flaky cluster into a\n39\t/// local stampede — the meltdown gantry exists to prevent.\n40\tpub const DEFAULT_MAX_LOCAL_FALLBACKS: u32 = 1;\n41\t\n42\t/// The cap on infra-driven local fallbacks (plan §\"Local execution & caps\").\n43\t#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\n44\tpub struct FallbackPolicy {\n45\t    /// Maximum number of local fallback runs a single invocation may take.\n46\t    pub max_local_fallbacks: u32,\n47\t}\n48\t\n49\timpl FallbackPolicy {\n50\t    /// The default policy: one capped local run per invocation.\n51\t    pub fn capped() -> Self {\n52\t        FallbackPolicy {\n53\t            max_local_fallbacks: DEFAULT_MAX_LOCAL_FALLBACKS,\n54\t        }\n55\t    }\n56\t\n57\t    /// A policy with an explicit cap (0 disables local fallback entirely).\n58\t    pub fn with_cap(max_local_fallbacks: u32) -> Self {\n59\t        FallbackPolicy {\n60\t            max_local_fallbacks,\n61\t        }\n62\t    }\n63\t\n64\t    /// A policy that never falls back locally — an infra failure is terminal.\n65\t    ///\n66\t    /// For callers that want the infra classification surfaced without a local run\n67\t    /// behind it (a fire drill, a CI box with no toolchain, this module's tests).\n68\t    pub fn no_fallback() -> Self {\n69\t        FallbackPolicy::with_cap(0)\n70\t    }\n71\t\n72\t    /// True iff another local fallback is within budget.\n73\t    pub fn allows(&self, fallbacks_used: u32) -> bool {\n74\t        fallbacks_used < self.max_local_fallbacks\n75\t    }\n76\t}\n77\t\n78\timpl Default for FallbackPolicy {\n79\t    fn default() -> Self {\n80\t        FallbackPolicy::capped()\n81\t    }\n82\t}\n83\t\n84\t/// What to do with a verdict — the ladder's output.\n85\t///\n86\t/// This is a pure function of (verdict, fallbacks already used, policy); see\n87\t/// [`decide_retry`]. Keeping it a value rather than a control-flow side effect is\n88\t/// what makes INV-7 testable: \"a TestFailure never yields [`RetryDecision::LocalFallback`]\"\n89\t/// is an assertion over the whole enum, not a review of every call site.\n90\t#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\n91\tpub enum RetryDecision {\n92\t    /// The verdict is final. Exit with this code; run nothing locally.\n93\t    Terminal {\n94\t        /// The caller's exit code (INV-3 exit-code fidelity).\n95\t        exit_code: i32,\n96\t    },\n97\t    /// An infra failure with budget left: run locally; the local exit code wins.\n98\t    LocalFallback {\n99\t        /// Human-readable infra reason, printed before the local run starts.\n100\t        reason: String,\n101\t        /// 1-based index of this fallback attempt.\n102\t        attempt: u32,\n103\t    },\n104\t    /// An infra failure with the fallback cap already spent: exit non-zero, loudly.\n105\t    FallbackExhausted {\n106\t        /// Human-readable infra reason.\n107\t        reason: String,\n108\t        /// The caller's exit code (non-zero — no verdict was ever produced).\n109\t        exit_code: i32,\n110\t    },\n111\t}\n112\t\n113\t/// Apply the verdict ladder (plan §\"Verdict semantics\", DD-4, INV-7).\n114\t///\n115\t/// Only [`Verdict::InfraFailure`] can yield a local run, and only while the\n116\t/// [`FallbackPolicy`] budget lasts. Every other verdict — including both real\n117\t/// failure kinds — is terminal by construction: the match has no arm that could\n118\t/// route a `TestFailure` or a `GateFailure` to [`RetryDecision::LocalFallback`],\n119\t/// which is INV-7 enforced by the type system rather than by discipline.\n120\tpub fn decide_retry(\n121\t    verdict: &Verdict,\n122\t    fallbacks_used: u32,\n123\t    policy: &FallbackPolicy,\n124\t) -> RetryDecision {\n125\t    match verdict {\n126\t        // No verdict was produced remotely, so producing one locally is strictly\n127\t        // better (DD-4) — while the cap allows it.\n128\t        Verdict::InfraFailure { reason } => {\n129\t            if policy.allows(fallbacks_used) {\n130\t                RetryDecision::LocalFallback {\n131\t                    reason: reason.clone(),\n132\t                    attempt: fallbacks_used + 1,\n133\t                }\n134\t            } else {\n135\t                RetryDecision::FallbackExhausted {\n136\t                    reason: reason.clone(),\n137\t                    exit_code: verdict.clone().to_exit_code(),\n138\t                }\n139\t            }\n140\t        }\n141\t        // Pass, TestFailure, GateFailure, Cancelled, Superseded: terminal. A real\n142\t        // failure is never re-rolled locally (INV-7) — rerunning would waste the\n143\t        // machine gantry protects and risks masking a real failure with an\n144\t        // environment-dependent local pass.\n145\t        terminal => RetryDecision::Terminal {\n146\t            exit_code: terminal.clone().to_exit_code(),\n147\t        },\n148\t    }\n149\t}\n150\t\n151\t/// Run the decision pipeline for an intercepted subcommand.\n152\t///\n153\t/// This is the core remote execution path (plan §\"Architecture\"):\n154\t/// 1. Check GitGate eligibility — ineligible degrades to a local run with the reason.\n155\t/// 2. Push epoch ref via RefPusher — a push failure is an InfraFailure (plan §4).\n156\t/// 3. Submit to the backend, then wait for the authoritative verdict.\n157\t/// 4. Apply the verdict ladder ([`decide_retry`]) and return the faithful exit code.\n158\t///\n159\t/// ## Parameters\n160\t///\n161\t/// - `config`: The hardcoded Config.\n162\t/// - `tool`: Tool name (e.g., \"cargo\").\n163\t/// - `subcommand`: Subcommand (e.g., \"test\").\n164\t/// - `args`: The command arguments (e.g., [\"--\", \"--nocapture\"]).\n165\t/// - `repo_url`: The repository URL (file:// for local testing).\n166\t/// - `sha`: The commit SHA to run.\n167\t/// - `cwd_rel`: Caller's directory relative to repo root.\n168\t///\n169\t/// ## Returns\n170\t///\n171\t/// The exit code that faithfully represents the verdict (INV-3): 0 for Pass, the\n172\t/// preserved non-zero code for TestFailure/GateFailure, and — after an\n173\t/// InfraFailure — the *local* run's exit code, which wins by definition (DD-4).\n174\tpub fn run_remote(\n175\t    config: &Config,\n176\t    tool: &str,\n177\t    subcommand: &str,\n178\t    args: &[String],\n179\t    repo_url: &str,\n180\t    sha: &str,\n181\t    cwd_rel: PathBuf,\n182\t) -> i32 {\n183\t    let policy = config.fallback_policy();\n184\t\n185\t    // Step 1: GitGate. Ineligibility never went remote at all, so there is no\n186\t    // verdict to ladder — it degrades straight to the capped local run, with the\n187\t    // reason printed (plan §\"fallback / degradation\": infra failure OR gate\n188\t    // ineligibility). Exiting non-zero here would fake a test failure out of a\n189\t    // dirty working tree.\n190\t    let eligibility = crate::gate::check_git_gate(&config.ci_remote);\n191\t    if !eligibility.eligible {\n192\t        eprintln!(\"[gantry] decision: local ({})\", eligibility.reason);\n193\t        return run_local(config, subcommand, args, &eligibility.reason);\n194\t    }\n195\t\n196\t    eprintln!(\"[gantry] decision: remote execution eligible\");\n197\t\n198\t    // Steps 2-3: everything from here to the verdict is the remote attempt, and\n199\t    // every way it can go wrong is an InfraFailure — never a test failure (DD-4:\n200\t    // \"pod-never-started is exit 1, not fallback\" is the bug being fixed here).\n201\t    let verdict = attempt_remote(config, tool, subcommand, args, repo_url, sha, cwd_rel);\n202\t\n203\t    eprintln!(\"[gantry] verdict: {}\", verdict);\n204\t\n205\t    // Step 4: the ladder.\n206\t    match decide_retry(&verdict, 0, &policy) {\n207\t        RetryDecision::Terminal { exit_code } => exit_code,\n208\t        RetryDecision::LocalFallback { reason, attempt } => {\n209\t            eprintln!(\n210\t                \"[gantry] fallback: local run after infra failure ({reason}) [{attempt}/{}]\",\n211\t                policy.max_local_fallbacks\n212\t            );\n213\t            run_local(config, subcommand, args, &reason)\n214\t        }\n215\t        RetryDecision::FallbackExhausted { reason, exit_code } => {\n216\t            eprintln!(\"[gantry] fallback exhausted: {reason} (local fallback disabled)\");\n217\t            exit_code\n218\t        }\n219\t    }\n220\t}\n221\t\n222\t/// Run the remote attempt and classify its outcome as a [`Verdict`].\n223\t///\n224\t/// Push, submit, and wait failures all produce no verdict about the *code*, so\n225\t/// each becomes an [`Verdict::InfraFailure`] carrying its reason (plan §4 \"Push\n226\t/// failure = InfraFailure → local fallback\"). Only the backend's own `wait` can\n227\t/// return Pass / TestFailure / GateFailure — the verdict is read from the backend's\n228\t/// status flow (exit code, or a `verdict.json` when the executor emits one), never\n229\t/// from parsing log text (INV-3).\n230\tfn attempt_remote(\n231\t    config: &Config,\n232\t    tool: &str,\n233\t    subcommand: &str,\n234\t    args: &[String],\n235\t    repo_url: &str,\n236\t    sha: &str,\n237\t    cwd_rel: PathBuf,\n238\t) -> Verdict {\n239\t    // Step 2: push the epoch ref so the remote has the content to check out.\n240\t    let push_result = RefPusher::push(config, sha);\n241\t    if !push_result.success {\n242\t        return Verdict::infra_failure(format!(\"push failed: {}\", push_result.reason));\n243\t    }\n244\t\n245\t    // Step 3: submit.\n246\t    let backend = CommandBackend::default_config();\n247\t    let spec = RunSpec::new(tool, subcommand, args.to_vec(), repo_url, sha, cwd_rel);\n248\t\n249\t    let handle = match backend.submit(&spec) {\n250\t        Ok(h) => h,\n251\t        Err(e) => return Verdict::infra_failure(format!(\"submit failed: {}\", e)),\n252\t    };\n253\t\n254\t    match &handle {\n255\t        RunHandle::Command { handle: h } => eprintln!(\"[gantry] submitted: {}\", h),\n256\t        RunHandle::Argo { workflow_name, .. } => eprintln!(\"[gantry] submitted: {}\", workflow_name),\n257\t    }\n258\t\n259\t    // Step 3b: wait for the authoritative verdict.\n260\t    let deadline = Instant::now() + config.deadline();\n261\t    match backend.wait(&handle, deadline) {\n262\t        Ok(v) => v,\n263\t        Err(e) => Verdict::infra_failure(format!(\"wait failed: {}\", e)),\n264\t    }\n265\t}\n266\t\n267\t/// Run the command locally and return its exit code faithfully (INV-3).\n268\t///\n269\t/// The bottom rung of the ladder (DD-4): reached after an InfraFailure, or when\n270\t/// GitGate ruled the tree ineligible. Resolves the real binary through\n271\t/// [`shim::resolve_real_binary`] — so the self-recursion guard applies here exactly\n272\t/// as it does on the passthrough path — and forwards `[subcommand, args...]` as an\n273\t/// argv array, never a shell string (S-4, INV-5).\n274\t///\n275\t/// `reason` is printed before the run so the transcript always says why the local\n276\t/// run happened; a resolution or spawn failure is loud and non-zero, never a\n277\t/// silent skip (INV-1).\n278\t///\n279\t/// The cgroup resource cap (DD-9) and the fallback admission semaphore (plan\n280\t/// §\"Local execution & caps\") wrap this call in their own Phase 1b beads; the\n281\t/// attempt cap that makes this a *capped* fallback is [`FallbackPolicy`], applied\n282\t/// by [`decide_retry`] before we get here.\n283\tpub fn run_local(config: &Config, subcommand: &str, args: &[String], reason: &str) -> i32 {\n284\t    eprintln!(\"[gantry] local: running `{subcommand}` locally ({reason})\");\n285\t\n286\t    let real = match shim::resolve_real_binary(config) {\n287\t        Ok(path) => path,\n288\t        Err(why) => {\n289\t            eprintln!(\"[gantry] {why}\");\n290\t            return 1;\n291\t        }\n292\t    };\n293\t\n294\t    match std::process::Command::new(&real)\n295\t        .arg(subcommand)\n296\t        .args(args)\n297\t        .status()\n298\t    {\n299\t        Ok(status) => exit_code_of(status),\n300\t        Err(why) => {\n301\t            eprintln!(\"[gantry] failed to run `{}`: {why}\", real.display());\n302\t            1\n303\t        }\n304\t    }\n305\t}\n306\t\n307\t/// Map a child's exit status to an exit code faithfully (INV-3).\n308\t///\n309\t/// A normal exit yields its code; on Unix, death by signal `S` yields `128 + S`\n310\t/// (the shell's `$?` convention), matching [`crate::shim`]'s passthrough mapping so\n311\t/// a fallback run is indistinguishable from an unshimmed one. A platform that\n312\t/// withholds a code defaults to 1 rather than a silent success.\n313\tfn exit_code_of(status: std::process::ExitStatus) -> i32 {\n314\t    #[cfg(unix)]\n315\t    {\n316\t        use std::os::unix::process::ExitStatusExt;\n317\t        if let Some(signal) = status.signal() {\n318\t            return 128 + signal;\n319\t        }\n320\t    }\n321\t    status.code().unwrap_or(1)\n322\t}\n323\t\n324\t#[cfg(test)]\n325\tmod tests {\n326\t    use super::*;\n327\t\n328\t    // --- the ladder: one rung per verdict ------------------------------------\n329\t\n330\t    #[test]\n331\t    fn pass_is_terminal_with_exit_zero() {\n332\t        assert_eq!(\n333\t            decide_retry(&Verdict::Pass, 0, &FallbackPolicy::capped()),\n334\t            RetryDecision::Terminal { exit_code: 0 },\n335\t        );\n336\t    }\n337\t\n338\t    #[test]\n339\t    fn test_failure_is_terminal_and_preserves_its_exit_code() {\n340\t        // The remote ran the suite and it failed: exit non-zero, full stop.\n341\t        assert_eq!(\n342\t }\n645\t}\n646\t"}]},"uuid":"bb9556b5-c3fe-4511-9c84-cbbf32d178c6","timestamp":"2026-07-31T01:59:46.729Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/coding/gantry-rs/src/decision.rs","content":"// gantry — decision engine, verdict ladder, and retry semantics (plan §\"Verdict\n// semantics\", DD-4, INV-7).\n//\n// The pipeline stages (GitGate → RefPusher → backend submit/wait) each end in a\n// [`Verdict`]; this module is where a verdict becomes an *action*. The ladder is\n// the load-bearing safety property of the whole tool:\n//\n//   Pass          → exit 0.\n//   TestFailure   → exit non-zero, full stop. NEVER a local rerun (INV-7).\n//   GateFailure   → exit non-zero, attributed to the named gate. NEVER a local\n//                   rerun (INV-7) — an agent must fix the lint, not reroll dice.\n//   InfraFailure  → no verdict was produced, so produce one locally: a capped\n//                   local run whose exit code wins (DD-4).\n//   Cancelled     → exit 130, no rerun (the user asked for it to stop).\n//   Superseded    → a newer sha owns the answer; no rerun.\n//\n// \"Capped\" has two independent meanings in the plan and both are honored here as\n// far as this phase reaches: the *attempt* cap lives in [`FallbackPolicy`] (a\n// remote outage degrades to at most N local runs per invocation, default 1 — an\n// infra failure can never become a retry loop), and the *resource* cap (cgroup\n// slice, DD-9) plus the fallback admission semaphore land in their own Phase 1b\n// beads (bf-xj0, bf-299) and hook into [`run_local`] when they do.\n//\n// Every rung logs why it was taken: \"silently skipped a test run\" must be an\n// unrepresentable state (DD-4).\n\nuse crate::backend::command::CommandBackend;\nuse crate::backend::{RemoteBackend, RunHandle, RunSpec, Verdict};\nuse crate::config::Config;\nuse crate::refs::RefPusher;\nuse crate::shim;\nuse std::path::PathBuf;\nuse std::time::Instant;\n\n/// How many local runs an infra failure may degrade into, per invocation.\n///\n/// One: an infra failure buys exactly one honest local answer. Zero would make a\n/// backend outage block builds; more than one would turn a flaky cluster into a\n/// local stampede — the meltdown gantry exists to prevent.\npub const DEFAULT_MAX_LOCAL_FALLBACKS: u32 = 1;\n\n/// The cap on infra-driven local fallbacks (plan §\"Local execution & caps\").\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct FallbackPolicy {\n    /// Maximum number of local fallback runs a single invocation may take.\n    pub max_local_fallbacks: u32,\n}\n\nimpl FallbackPolicy {\n    /// The default policy: one capped local run per invocation.\n    pub fn capped() -> Self {\n        FallbackPolicy {\n            max_local_fallbacks: DEFAULT_MAX_LOCAL_FALLBACKS,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// A policy with an explicit cap (0 disables local fallback entirely).\n    pub fn with_cap(max_local_fallbacks: u32) -> Self {\n        FallbackPolicy {\n            max_local_fallbacks,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// A policy that never falls back locally — an infra failure is terminal.\n    ///\n    /// For callers that want the infra classification surfaced without a local run\n    /// behind it (a fire drill, a CI box with no toolchain, this module's tests).\n    pub fn no_fallback() -> Self {\n        FallbackPolicy::with_cap(0)\n    }\n\n    /// True iff another local fallback is within budget.\n    pub fn allows(&self, fallbacks_used: u32) -> bool {\n        fallbacks_used < self.max_local_fallbacks\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Default for FallbackPolicy {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        FallbackPolicy::capped()\n    }\n}\n\n/// What to do with a verdict — the ladder's output.\n///\n/// This is a pure function of (verdict, fallbacks already used, policy); see\n/// [`decide_retry`]. Keeping it a value rather than a control-flow side effect is\n/// what makes INV-7 testable: \"a TestFailure never yields [`RetryDecision::LocalFallback`]\"\n/// is an assertion over the whole enum, not a review of every call site.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum RetryDecision {\n    /// The verdict is final. Exit with this code; run nothing locally.\n    Terminal {\n        /// The caller's exit code (INV-3 exit-code fidelity).\n        exit_code: i32,\n    },\n    /// An infra failure with budget left: run locally; the local exit code wins.\n    LocalFallback {\n        /// Human-readable infra reason, printed before the local run starts.\n        reason: String,\n        /// 1-based index of this fallback attempt.\n        attempt: u32,\n    },\n    /// An infra failure with the fallback cap already spent: exit non-zero, loudly.\n    FallbackExhausted {\n        /// Human-readable infra reason.\n        reason: String,\n        /// The caller's exit code (non-zero — no verdict was ever produced).\n        exit_code: i32,\n    },\n}\n\n/// Apply the verdict ladder (plan §\"Verdict semantics\", DD-4, INV-7).\n///\n/// Only [`Verdict::InfraFailure`] can yield a local run, and only while the\n/// [`FallbackPolicy`] budget lasts. Every other verdict — including both real\n/// failure kinds — is terminal by construction: the match has no arm that could\n/// route a `TestFailure` or a `GateFailure` to [`RetryDecision::LocalFallback`],\n/// which is INV-7 enforced by the type system rather than by discipline.\npub fn decide_retry(\n    verdict: &Verdict,\n    fallbacks_used: u32,\n    policy: &FallbackPolicy,\n) -> RetryDecision {\n    match verdict {\n        // No verdict was produced remotely, so producing one locally is strictly\n        // better (DD-4) — while the cap allows it.\n        Verdict::InfraFailure { reason } => {\n            if policy.allows(fallbacks_used) {\n                RetryDecision::LocalFallback {\n                    reason: reason.clone(),\n                    attempt: fallbacks_used + 1,\n                }\n            } else {\n                RetryDecision::FallbackExhausted {\n                    reason: reason.clone(),\n                    exit_code: verdict.clone().to_exit_code(),\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        // Pass, TestFailure, GateFailure, Cancelled, Superseded: terminal. A real\n        // failure is never re-rolled locally (INV-7) — rerunning would waste the\n        // machine gantry protects and risks masking a real failure with an\n        // environment-dependent local pass.\n        terminal => RetryDecision::Terminal {\n            exit_code: terminal.clone().to_exit_code(),\n        },\n    }\n}\n\n/// Run the decision pipeline for an intercepted subcommand.\n///\n/// This is the core remote execution path (plan §\"Architecture\"):\n/// 1. Check GitGate eligibility — ineligible degrades to a local run with the reason.\n/// 2. Push epoch ref via RefPusher — a push failure is an InfraFailure (plan §4).\n/// 3. Submit to the backend, then wait for the authoritative verdict.\n/// 4. Apply the verdict ladder ([`decide_retry`]) and return the faithful exit code.\n///\n/// ## Parameters\n///\n/// - `config`: The hardcoded Config.\n/// - `tool`: Tool name (e.g., \"cargo\").\n/// - `subcommand`: Subcommand (e.g., \"test\").\n/// - `args`: The command arguments (e.g., [\"--\", \"--nocapture\"]).\n/// - `repo_url`: The repository URL (file:// for local testing).\n/// - `sha`: The commit SHA to run.\n/// - `cwd_rel`: Caller's directory relative to repo root.\n///\n/// ## Returns\n///\n/// The exit code that faithfully represents the verdict (INV-3): 0 for Pass, the\n/// preserved non-zero code for TestFailure/GateFailure, and — after an\n/// InfraFailure — the *local* run's exit code, which wins by definition (DD-4).\npub fn run_remote(\n    config: &Config,\n    tool: &str,\n    subcommand: &str,\n    args: &[String],\n    repo_url: &str,\n    sha: &str,\n    cwd_rel: PathBuf,\n) -> i32 {\n    let policy = config.fallback_policy();\n\n    // Step 1: GitGate. Ineligibility never went remote at all, so there is no\n    // verdict to ladder — it degrades straight to the capped local run, with the\n    // reason printed (plan §\"fallback / degradation\": infra failure OR gate\n    // ineligibility). Exiting non-zero here would fake a test failure out of a\n    // dirty working tree.\n    let eligibility = crate::gate::check_git_gate(&config.ci_remote);\n    if !eligibility.eligible {\n        eprintln!(\"[gantry] decision: local ({})\", eligibility.reason);\n        return run_local(config, subcommand, args, &eligibility.reason);\n    }\n\n    eprintln!(\"[gantry] decision: remote execution eligible\");\n\n    // Steps 2-3: everything from here to the verdict is the remote attempt, and\n    // every way it can go wrong is an InfraFailure — never a test failure (DD-4:\n    // \"pod-never-started is exit 1, not fallback\" is the bug being fixed here).\n    let verdict = attempt_remote(config, tool, subcommand, args, repo_url, sha, cwd_rel);\n\n    eprintln!(\"[gantry] verdict: {}\", verdict);\n\n    // Step 4: the ladder.\n    match decide_retry(&verdict, 0, &policy) {\n        RetryDecision::Terminal { exit_code } => exit_code,\n        RetryDecision::LocalFallback { reason, attempt } => {\n            eprintln!(\n                \"[gantry] fallback: local run after infra failure ({reason}) [{attempt}/{}]\",\n                policy.max_local_fallbacks\n            );\n            run_local(config, subcommand, args, &reason)\n        }\n        RetryDecision::FallbackExhausted { reason, exit_code } => {\n            eprintln!(\"[gantry] fallback exhausted: {reason} (local fallback disabled)\");\n            exit_code\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Run the remote attempt and classify its outcome as a [`Verdict`].\n///\n/// Push, submit, and wait failures all produce no verdict about the *code*, so\n/// each becomes an [`Verdict::InfraFailure`] carrying its reason (plan §4 \"Push\n/// failure = InfraFailure → local fallback\"). Only the backend's own `wait` can\n/// return Pass / TestFailure / GateFailure — the verdict is read from the backend's\n/// status flow (exit code, or a `verdict.json` when the executor emits one), never\n/// from parsing log text (INV-3).\nfn attempt_remote(\n    config: &Config,\n    tool: &str,\n    subcommand: &str,\n    args: &[String],\n    repo_url: &str,\n    sha: &str,\n    cwd_rel: PathBuf,\n) -> Verdict {\n    // Step 2: push the epoch ref so the remote has the content to check out.\n    let push_result = RefPusher::push(config, sha);\n    if !push_result.success {\n        return Verdict::infra_failure(format!(\"push failed: {}\", push_result.reason));\n    }\n\n    // Step 3: submit.\n    let backend = CommandBackend::default_config();\n    let spec = RunSpec::new(tool, subcommand, args.to_vec(), repo_url, sha, cwd_rel);\n\n    let handle = match backend.submit(&spec) {\n        Ok(h) => h,\n        Err(e) => return Verdict::infra_failure(format!(\"submit failed: {}\", e)),\n    };\n\n    match &handle {\n        RunHandle::Command { handle: h } => eprintln!(\"[gantry] submitted: {}\", h),\n        RunHandle::Argo { workflow_name, .. } => eprintln!(\"[gantry] submitted: {}\", workflow_name),\n    }\n\n    // Step 3b: wait for the authoritative verdict.\n    let deadline = Instant::now() + config.deadline();\n    match backend.wait(&handle, deadline) {\n        Ok(v) => v,\n        Err(e) => Verdict::infra_failure(format!(\"wait failed: {}\", e)),\n    }\n}\n\n/// Run the command locally and return its exit code faithfully (INV-3).\n///\n/// The bottom rung of the ladder (DD-4): reached after an InfraFailure, or when\n/// GitGate ruled the tree ineligible. Resolves the real binary through\n/// [`shim::resolve_real_binary`] — so the self-recursion guard applies here exactly\n/// as it does on the passthrough path — and forwards `[subcommand, args...]` as an\n/// argv array, never a shell string (S-4, INV-5).\n///\n/// `reason` is printed before the run so the transcript always says why the local\n/// run happened; a resolution or spawn failure is loud and non-zero, never a\n/// silent skip (INV-1).\n///\n/// The cgroup resource cap (DD-9) and the fallback admission semaphore (plan\n/// §\"Local execution & caps\") wrap this call in their own Phase 1b beads; the\n/// attempt cap that makes this a *capped* fallback is [`FallbackPolicy`], applied\n/// by [`decide_retry`] before we get here.\npub fn run_local(config: &Config, subcommand: &str, args: &[String], reason: &str) -> i32 {\n    eprintln!(\"[gantry] local: running `{subcommand}` locally ({reason})\");\n\n    let real = match shim::resolve_real_binary(config) {\n        Ok(path) => path,\n        Err(why) => {\n            eprintln!(\"[gantry] {why}\");\n            return 1;\n        }\n    };\n\n    match std::process::Command::new(&real)\n        .arg(subcommand)\n        .args(args)\n        .status()\n    {\n        Ok(status) => exit_code_of(status),\n        Err(why) => {\n            eprintln!(\"[gantry] failed to run `{}`: {why}\", real.display());\n            1\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Map a child's exit status to an exit code faithfully (INV-3).\n///\n/// A normal exit yields its code; on Unix, death by signal `S` yields `128 + S`\n/// (the shell's `$?` convention), matching [`crate::shim`]'s passthrough mapping so\n/// a fallback run is indistinguishable from an unshimmed one. A platform that\n/// withholds a code defaults to 1 rather than a silent success.\nfn exit_code_of(status: std::process::ExitStatus) -> i32 {\n    #[cfg(unix)]\n    {\n        use std::os::unix::process::ExitStatusExt;\n        if let Some(signal) = status.signal() {\n            return 128 + signal;\n        }\n    }\n    status.code().unwrap_or(1)\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    // --- the ladder: one rung per verdict ------------------------------------\n\n    #[test]\n    fn pass_is_terminal_with_exit_zero() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            decide_retry(&Verdict::Pass, 0, &FallbackPolicy::capped()),\n            RetryDecision::Terminal { exit_code: 0 },\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_failure_is_terminal_and_preserves_its_exit_code() {\n        // The remote ran the suite and it failed: exit non-zero, full stop.\n        assert_eq!(\n            decide_retry(&Verdict::test_failure(101), 0, &FallbackPolicy::capped()),\n            RetryDecision::Terminal { exit_code: 101 },\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn gate_failure_is_terminal_and_preserves_its_exit_code() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            decide_retry(\n                &Verdict::gate_failure(\"clippy\".to_string(), 101),\n                0,\n                &FallbackPolicy::capped(),\n            ),\n            RetryDecision::Terminal { exit_code: 101 },\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn cancelled_is_terminal_at_130() {\n        // Ctrl-C is the user's decision, not an infra fault: no local rerun.\n        assert_eq!(\n            decide_retry(&Verdict::Cancelled, 0, &FallbackPolicy::capped()),\n            RetryDecision::Terminal { exit_code: 130 },\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn superseded_is_terminal() {\n        // A newer sha owns the answer; rerunning this one locally is pure waste.\n        assert_eq!(\n            decide_retry(\n                &Verdict::Superseded {\n                    newer_sha: \"deadbeef\".to_string()\n                },\n                0,\n                &FallbackPolicy::capped(),\n            ),\n            RetryDecision::Terminal { exit_code: 0 },\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn infra_failure_falls_back_locally_carrying_its_reason() {\n        // DD-4: no verdict was produced, so produce one locally. The reason must\n        // survive into the decision so the transcript can print it.\n        let verdict = Verdict::infra_failure(\"pod was OOMKilled\".to_string());\n        assert_eq!(\n            decide_retry(&verdict, 0, &FallbackPolicy::capped()),\n            RetryDecision::LocalFallback {\n                reason: \"pod was OOMKilled\".to_string(),\n                attempt: 1,\n            },\n        );\n    }\n\n    // --- INV-7: real failures never retry locally ----------------------------\n\n    #[test]\n    fn inv7_test_and_gate_failures_never_fall_back_under_any_budget() {\n        // INV-7 is a property over the whole (verdict, budget) space, not a\n        // property of one call site: no budget, however generous, and no number of\n        // prior fallbacks may turn a real failure into a local rerun.\n        let real_failures = [\n            Verdict::test_failure(1),\n            Verdict::test_failure(101),\n            Verdict::gate_failure(\"clippy\".to_string(), 1),\n            Verdict::gate_failure(\"fmt\".to_string(), 101),\n        ];\n        for verdict in &real_failures {\n            for used in 0..4u32 {\n                for cap in 0..4u32 {\n                    let decision = decide_retry(verdict, used, &FallbackPolicy::with_cap(cap));\n                    assert!(\n                        matches!(decision, RetryDecision::Terminal { exit_code } if exit_code != 0),\n                        \"INV-7: {verdict} must be terminal and non-zero (used={used}, cap={cap}), got {decision:?}\",\n                    );\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn inv7_only_infra_failure_ever_yields_a_local_run() {\n        // The dual of the above: enumerate every verdict shape and assert that the\n        // only one reaching LocalFallback is InfraFailure.\n        let ladder = [\n            Verdict::Pass,\n            Verdict::test_failure(1),\n            Verdict::gate_failure(\"clippy\".to_string(), 1),\n   ];\n        for verdict in &ladder {\n            let falls_back = matches!(\n                decide_retry(verdict, 0, &FallbackPolicy::capped()),\n                RetryDecision::LocalFallback { .. },\n            );\n            assert_eq!(\n                falls_back,\n                verdict.should_fallback(),\n                \"{verdict}: decide_retry and Verdict::should_fallback must 0, &FallbackPolicy::no_fallback()) {\n            RetryDecision::FallbackExhausted { reason, exit_code } => {\n                assert_eq!(reason, \"cluster unreachable\");\n                assert_ne!(exit_code, 0, \"an infra failure may never exit 0\");\n            }\n            other => panic!(\"expected FallbackExhausted, got {other:?}\"),\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn Contract drift is an infra failure by\n38\t///   definition: the remote answered in a language we do not speak, so we have no\n39\t///   verdict, and guessing one would be worse than falling back (plan\n40\t///   §\"Versioning & compatibility\").\n41\t/// - `None` — no verdict.json at all; the caller applies the exit-code contract\n42\t///   (the documented graceful degradation for plain command templates).\n43\tfn parse_verdict_stdout(stdout: &str) -> Option<Verdict> {\n44\t    let candidate = stdout\n45\t        .lines()\n46\t        .rev()\n47\t        .map(str::trim)\n48\t        .find(|line| line.starts_with('{') && line.contains(\"schema_version\"))?;\n49\t\n50\t    match VerdictJson::parse(candidate) {\n51\t        Ok(Some(verdict)) => Some(verdict.to_verdict()),\n52\t        // `parse` returns Ok(None) for no verdict it can hand back at all; treat it\n53\t        // like an absent file rather than inventing a classification.\n54\t        Ok(None) => None,\n55\t        Err(why) => Some(Verdict::infra_failure(format!(\n56\t            \"verdict.json contract drift: {why}\"\n57\t        ))),\n58\t    }\n59\t}\n60\t\n61\t/// Configuration for the command-template backend.\n62\t#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n63\tpub struct CommandConfig {\n64\t    /// Submit argv array with placeholders.\n65\t    pub submit: Vec<String>,\n66\t    /// Wait argv array with placeholders \"./contrib/gantry-exec.sh\".to_string(),\n86\t                \"wait\".to_string(),\n87\t                \"{handle}\".to_string(),\n88\t            ],\n89\t            logs: None,\n90\t {handle} with their values.\n117\t    /// Substitution happens at the argv level (no shell interpolation per S-4).\n118\t    fn substitute_argv(argv: &[String], spec: &RunSpec, handle: Option<&str>) -> Vec<String> {\n119\t        let args_json = serde_json::to_string(&spec.args).unwrap_or_else(|_| \"[]\".to_string());\n120\t\n121\t        argv.iter()\n122\t            .map(|arg| {\n123\t                let mut result = arg.clone();\n124\t                result = result.replace(\"{repo}\", &spec.repo_url);\n125\t                result = result.replace(\"{rev}\", &spec.sha);\n126\t                result = result.replace(\"{args_json}\", &args_json);\n127\t                if let Some(h) = handle {\n128\t                    result = result.replace(\"{handle}\", h);\n129\t                }\n130\t                result\n131\t            })\n132\t            .collect()\n133\t    }\n134\t\n135\t    /// Run a command and return its output.\n136\t    fn run_command(&self, argv: &[String]) -> Result<Output, BackendError> {\n137\t        if argv.is_empty() {\n138\t            return Err(BackendError::new(\"empty argv array\"));\n139\t        }\n140\t\n141\t        let (exe, args) = argv.split_first().unwrap();\n142\t        Command::new(exe)\n143\t            .args(args)\n144\t            .output()\n145\t            .map_err(|e| BackendError::new(&format!(\"failed to run {\n150\t        if argv.is_empty() {\n151\t            return Err(BackendError::new(\"empty argv array\"));\n152\t        }\n153\t\n154\t        let (exe, args) = argv.split_first().unwrap();\n155\t .spawn()\n160\t            .map_err(|e| BackendError::new(&format!(\"failed to spawn logs command: {}\", e)))?;\n161\t\n162\t        // Stream stdout to the writer\n163\t        if let Some(stdout) = BackendError::new(&format!(\"failed to read log line: {}\", e)))?;\n167\t                writeln!(out, \"{}\", line)\n168\t                    .map_err(|e| BackendError::new(&format!(\"failed to write .map_err(|e| BackendError::new(&format!(\"failed to wait for logs command: {}\", e)))?;\n176\t\n177\t        if !status.success() {\n178\t            // Log streaming is best-effort — don't fail the run if logs fail\n179\t            eprintln!(\"[gantry] warning: logs command exited with status: {:?}\", status.code());\n180\t        }\n181\t\n182\t        Ok(())\n183\t    }\n184\t}\n185\t\n186\timpl Default for CommandBackend {\n187\t    fn default() -> Self {\n188\t        Self::default_config()\n189\t    }\n190\t}\n191\t\n192\timpl RemoteBackend for CommandBackend {\n193\t    /// Submit a run to the executor.\n194\t    ///\n195\t    /// Runs the configured submit argv with placeholder substitution:\n196\t    /// - {repo} -> repository URL\n197\t    /// - {rev} -> commit SHA\n198\t    /// - {args_json} -> JSON array of command arguments\n199\t    ///\n200\t    /// The command's stdout is the handle (an opaque string that wait() can use).\n201\t    /// The handle is trimmed of whitespace and should be a single line.\n202\t    ///\n203\t    /// ## Errors\n204\t    ///\n205\t    /// Returns Err if:\n206\t    /// - The submit command cannot be run\n207\t    /// - The submit command exits non-zero (submit failure)\n208\t    /// - The command emits no output on stdout\n209\t    fn submit(&self, spec: &RunSpec) -> Result<RunHandle, BackendError> {\n210\t        let argv = Self::substitute_argv(&self.config.submit, spec, None);\n211\t\n212\t        let output = self.run_command(&argv)?;\n213\t\n214\t        if !output.status.success() {\n215\t            return Err(BackendError::new(&format!(\n216\t                \"submit command failed: {}\",\n217\t                String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)\n218\t            )));\n219\t        }\n220\t\n221\t        let handle = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();\n222\t        if handle.is_empty() {\n223\t            return Err(BackendError::new(\"submit command emitted empty handle\"));\n224\t        }\n225\t\n226\t        Ok(RunHandle::command(&handle))\n227\t    }\n228\t\n229\t    /// Stream logs from the running run.\n230\t    ///\n231\t    /// Phase 1a: if logs argv is configured, stream logs from the command.\n232\t    /// Otherwise, returns an error (not a panic — allows graceful degradation).\n233\t    fn stream_logs(&self, h: &RunHandle, out: &mut dyn Write) -> Result<(), BackendError> {\n234\t        let handle_str = match h {\n235\t            RunHandle::Command { handle } => handle,\n236\t            _ => {\n237\t                return Err(BackendError::new(\"command backend received non-Command handle\"))\n238\t            }\n239\t        };\n240\t\n241\t        let logs_argv = self.config.logs.as_ref()\n242\t            .ok_or_else(|| BackendError::new(\"logs argv not configured\"))?;\n243\t\n244\t        let argv = Self::substitute_argv(logs_argv, &RunSpec::placeholder(), Some(handle_str));\n245\t\n246\t        self.stream_command_logs(&argv, out)\n247\t    }\n248\t\n249\t    /// Wait for the run to complete and return its verdict.\n250\t    ///\n251\t    /// Runs the configured wait argv with placeholder substitution:\n252\t    /// - {handle} -> opaque handle string from submit()\n253\t    ///\n254\t    /// The verdict comes from the executor's *status*, never from parsing log text\n255\t    /// Contract drift is an infra failure by\n///   definition: the remote answered in a language we do not speak, so we have no\n///   verdict, and guessing one would be worse than falling back (plan\n///   §\"Versioning & compatibility\").\n/// - `None` — no verdict.json at all; the caller applies the exit-code contract\n///   (the documented graceful degradation for plain command templates).\nfn parse_verdict_stdout(stdout: &str) -> Option<Verdict> {\n    let candidate = stdout\n        .lines()\n        .rev()\n        .map(str::trim)\n        .find(|line| line.starts_with('{') && line.contains(\"schema_version\"))?;\n\n    match VerdictJson::parse(candidate) {\n        Ok(Some(verdict)) => Some(verdict.to_verdict()),\n        // `parse` returns Ok(None) for no verdict it can hand back at all; treat it\n        // like an absent file rather than inventing a classification.\n        Ok(None) => None,\n        Err(why) => Some(Verdict::infra_failure(format!(\n }\n    }\n}\n\n/// The command-template backend implementation.\npub struct CommandBackend {\n    /// Backend configuration.\n    config: CommandConfig,\n}\n\nimpl CommandBackend {\n    /// Create a &[String], spec: &RunSpec, handle: Option<&str>) -> Vec<String> {\n        let args_json = serde_json::to_string(&spec.args).unwrap_or_else(|_| \"[]\".to_string());\n\n        argv.iter()\n            .map(|arg| {\n                let mut result = arg.clone();\n                result = result.replace(\"{repo}\", &spec.repo_url);\n                result = result.replace(\"{rev}\", &spec.sha);\n                result = result.replace(\"{args_json}\", &args_json);\n                if let Some(h) = handle {\n                    result = result.replace(\"{handle}\", h);\n                }\n                result\n            })\n            .collect()\n    }\n\n    /// Run a command and return its output.\n    fn run_command(&self, argv: &[String]) -> Result<Output, BackendError> {\n        if .args(args)\n            .output()\n            .map_err(|e| BackendError::new(&format!(\"failed to run command: {}\", e)))\n    }\n\n    /// Stream logs from a command to a writer.\n    fn }\n\n        let (exe, args) = argv.split_first().unwrap();\n        let mut child = Command::new(exe)\n            .args(args)\n            .stdout(std::process::Stdio::piped())\n            .stderr(std::process::Stdio::piped())\n            .spawn()\n            .map_err(|e| BackendError::new(&format!(\"failed to spawn logs command: {}\", e)))?;\n\n        // Stream stdout to the This file joins them across the public API, driving a real\n// executor script through `submit` → `wait` → `decide_retry` so the property the\n// plan actually cares about is asserted on the whole path rather than on either\n// half:\n//\n//   - an infra failure (and only an infra failure) reaches a local run (DD-4),\n//   - a test or gate failure never does, whatever the fallback budget (INV-7),\n//   - and every rung's exit code survives to the caller (INV-3).\n//\n// The executor is the same shape as `contrib/gantry-exec.sh`: a script whose\n// `wait` prints a verdict.json (or not) and exits. That is the real status\n// surface, so these are contract tests, not mock theater.\n\nuse gantry::backend::command::{CommandBackend, CommandConfig};\nuse gantry::backend::{RemoteBackend, RunHandle, RunSpec, Verdict};\nuse gantry::decision::{decide_retry, FallbackPolicy, RetryDecision};\n\nuse std::io::Write;\nuse std::path::{Path, PathBuf};\nuse std::time::Instant;\n\n/// An executor whose `wait` behavior is keyed off the handle it is given.\n///\n/// One keyword per rung of the ladder:\n///\n/// | handle    | exit | stdout                          | verdict            |\n/// |-----------|------|---------------------------------|--------------------|\n/// | `pass`    | 0    | —                               | Pass               |\n/// | `fail`    | 1    | —                               | TestFailure(1)     |\n/// | `boom`    | 3    | —                               | InfraFailure       |\n/// | `oom`     | 1    | verdict.json `{oom:true}`       | InfraFailure       |\n/// | `deadline`| 1    | verdict.json `{deadline...}`    | InfraFailure       |\n/// | `clippy`  | 1    | verdict.json `{gate_failed}`    | GateFailure(clippy)|\n/// | `compile` | 1    | verdict.json `{failure_class}`  | TestFailure(101)   |\n/// | `future`  | 1    | verdict.json schema_version 99  | InfraFailure       |\nfn write_executor(dir: &Path) -> PathBuf {\n    let path = dir.join(\"ladder-exec.sh\");\n    let mut file = std::fs::File::create(&path).expect(\"create executor\");\n    file.write_all(\n        br#\"#!/usr/bin/env bash\ncase \"$1\" in\n    submit) echo \"handle-$3\"; exit 0 ;;\n    wait)\n        case \"$2\" in\n            *oom*)\n                echo '{\"schema_version\":1,\"phase\":\"Failed\",\"exit_code\":137,\"oom\":true}'; exit 1 ;;\n            *deadline*)\n                echo '{\"schema_version\":1,\"phase\":\"Failed\",\"exit_code\":1,\"deadline_exceeded\":true}'; exit 1 ;;\n            *clippy*)\n                echo '{\"schema_version\":1,\"phase\":\"Failed\",\"exit_code\":101,\"gate_failed\":\"clippy\"}'; exit 1 ;;\n            *compile*)\n                echo '{\"schema_version\":1,\"phase\":\"Failed\",\"exit_code\":101,\"failure_class\":\"compile_error\"}'; exit 1 ;;\n            *future*)\n                echo '{\"schema_version\":99,\"phase\":\"Succeeded\",\"exit_code\":0}'; exit 1 ;;\n            *boom*) exit 3 ;;\n            *pass*) exit 0 ;;\n            *)      exit 1 ;;\n        esac ;;\n    *) echo \"unknown: $1\" >&2; exit 2 ;;\nesac\n\"#,\n    )\n    .expect(\"write executor\");\n    path\n}\n\n/// A backend wired to the executor above.\n///\n/// Invoked as `bash <script>`: exec'ing a file another thread may still hold open\n/// for writing fails with ETXTBSY, and reading it costs nothing here.\nfn backend(executor: &Path) -> CommandBackend {\n    let exe = executor.to_str().expect(\"temp path is utf-8\").to_string();\n    CommandBackend::new(CommandConfig {\n        submit: vec![\n            \"bash\".to_string(),\n            exe.clone(),\n            \"submit\".to_string(),\n            \"{repo}\".to_string(),\n            \"{rev}\".to_string(),\n        ],\n        wait: vec![\n            \"bash\".to_string(),\n            exe,\n            \"wait\".to_string(),\n            \"{handle}\".to_string(),\n        ],\n        logs: None,\n        cancel: None,\n    })\n}\n\nfn spec(sha: &str) -> RunSpec {\n    RunSpec::new(\n        \"cargo\",\n        \"test\",\n        Vec::new(),\n        \"file:///repo\",\n        sha,\n        PathBuf::from(\".\"),\n    )\n}\n\n/// Drive one run all the way through: submit → wait → the ladder.\nfn run(executor: &Path, sha: &str, policy: &FallbackPolicy) -> (Verdict, RetryDecision) {\n    let backend = backend(executor);\n    let handle = backend.submit(&spec(sha)).expect(\"submit should succeed\");\n    assert!(\n        matches!(&handle, RunHandle::Command { handle } if handle.contains(sha)),\n        \"the handle must be the executor's stdout, got {handle:?}\",\n    );\n\n }\n}\n\n#[test]\nfn an_infra_failure_with_the_cap_spent_is_loud_and_non_zero() {\n    // A backend outage with no fallback budget left may not quietly exit 0: an\n    // absent verdict is never a With the cap spent, the\ndecision is `FallbackExhausted` — loud and non-zero, because an absent verdict\nmay never surface as success.\n\n## Backend status → verdict\n\nThe verdict comes from the backend's status surface, never from parsing log text\n(INV-3). Two shapes, in precedence order:\n\n1. **verdict.json** (`VerdictJson`, schema_version 1) — `oom` / `deadline_exceeded`\n   → `InfraFailure`; any `failure_class` → `TestFailure`; `gate_failed` (with no\n   failure class) → `GateFailure` attributed to that gate; otherwise phase +\n   exit code. A well-formed verdict.json this client cannot interpret (newer\n   schema) is contract drift → `InfraFailure`, never a guessed verdict.\n2. **Exit code only** — the universal contract for plain command templates:\n   0 → Pass, 1 → TestFailure, ≥2 (or a signal-killed executor) → InfraFailure.\n   A run that never happened is never reported as a test failure (DD-4).\n\n`CommandBackend::wait` prefers a verdict.json on stdout over the exit code;\n`ArgoBackend::wait` reads it from the workflow's output parameters.\n\n## Tests\n\n- `src/decision.rs` — one test per rung, the INV-7 property over\n  (verdict × used × cap), the cap/exhaustion behavior, and verdict.json →\n  decision cases.\n- `src/backend/command.rs` — the executor status contract (exit codes,\n  verdict.json precedence, gate attribution, schema drift, chatter).\n- `tests/verdict_ladder.rs` (new) — the whole path across the public API:\n  a real executor script through `submit` → `wait` → `decide_retry`, covering\n  every rung plus the INV-7 property driven from backend status.\n\n142 tests pass; `cargo fmt --check` and clippy are clean for these files.\n\n## Test-suite reliability fixed along the way\n\nThe suite was flaky in ways that made the ladder unverifiable:\n\n- `gate` and `refs` each held their own cwd mutex, so they serialized against\n  themselves but not each other — one module deleting a temp dir it had chdir'd\n  into made the other's `Command::spawn` fail with ENOENT.
